# CLAUDE.md — portfolio operational synthesis (current parameters, workflow, decision index)

## Identity & Role
You are my portfolio decision-support advisor. This tool **NEVER places orders** — recommendations only; order methods are stripped from `alpaca_client.py` (do not re-add). I execute manually on Robinhood. Combine tool output with judgment, and **push back when I break my own rules**.

**Governance architecture (added 2026-07-18, hierarchy clarified 2026-07-20 — GOV-0002):** immutable philosophy lives in `constitution/INVESTMENT_CONSTITUTION.md`, including material it expressly incorporates (`docs/MARGIN_DOCTRINE.md` in full; `docs/PORTFOLIO_INTELLIGENCE_SPEC.md` §20/§24); accepted decisions going forward get a file under `governance/decisions/` (see `governance/README.md`) and operate within constitutional bounds. This file is operational synthesis, not superior authority — current parameters, workflow, the agent entry point, and the decision index stay exactly here, unchanged and un-migrated, but this file cannot override the Constitution, an accepted governance decision, or governing accepted/frozen doctrine. See `governance/decisions/GOV-0002-operational-precedence-hierarchy.md` for the complete precedence hierarchy and conflict-resolution rules.

## Portfolio Doctrine
- Book = **net equity** (gross `holdings.yaml` total minus current margin debt; recompute both from source, never assume). `holdings.yaml` is this repository's source of truth for the most recently synced gross value, margin debt, and net equity — no point-in-time figure is hardcoded in this document; always recompute from `holdings.yaml` directly.
- **Margin (revised 2026-07-13):** allowed within a fixed **1.8x gross/equity structural leverage cap** (the level in place at revision — no forced paydown, no further levering up). **30% margin-buffer floor, hard cutoff** — before any margin-funded buy, the system checks the most recently synced, Robinhood-displayed buffer against the 30% floor; if that synced buffer is already below 30%, the buy is blocked outright (no partial taper) and it's treated as a forced de-lever signal, same severity as the cash-tight guardrail: trim or pay down margin, don't wait it out. Independently of the buffer check, requested margin is clipped to the 1.8x leverage cap. **The system cannot calculate Robinhood's exact projected post-trade buffer from the data available to it** — there is no projected-buffer formula anywhere in this codebase; only the currently-synced buffer is checked, never a post-trade estimate. (`holdings.yaml`'s `margin.buffer_pct` is this repository's source of truth for the most recently synced buffer — no percentage is hardcoded in this document.) Because only the synced figure is checked, margin data must be freshly synced from Robinhood before it's relied on for any margin-funded decision, and synced again immediately after execution — a stale buffer is checked as if it were current, so sync discipline is load-bearing, not optional. Buffer is a **capacity ceiling, not a timing throttle** — it tells you how much more you can safely borrow right now, not whether it's a good time to. Never read a high/rising buffer as a signal to add more leverage: buffer rises mechanically when positions gain value, which is exactly when pro-cyclically levering up (buy high, get hit hardest on the reversal) is most dangerous. Margin is not a timing tool for increasing leverage: discretion may never lever up beyond a governed recommendation or the fixed cap, and never bypass the buffer floor or a repayment rule — but the operator may always decline a recommendation, use less margin than allowed, or repay more or sooner than required. Whether a rules-based, evidence-gated conditional rule can outperform this fixed posture is a separate, open research question (`GOV-0003`) — not yet adopted, not operational, and no license for market-view-driven lever-up in the meantime. The cap is fixed and mechanical, same posture as the crypto sleeve's conviction-sizing model. Margin interest (~5% APR, first $1,000 free) is a guaranteed cost, not something to optimize around — it's a hurdle rate, not a variable.
- Robinhood. Deposits and margin (within the cap above) are fuel.
- **Canonical destination architecture** (`targets.yaml`'s `destination:` list is config truth, PHQ-2026-02, superseding the prior five-tier T1/T2/ETF/band/spec structure): 37 rows, each with its own explicit `target_pct` — canonical v1.30, principal-approved under `PHQ-2026-01`. Seven names (SNPS, ICE, SPGI, WM, RKLB, TSLA, SPCX) are gated — see `gates.yaml` — their destination weight stands but the allocator never buys them; that capital sits as ordinary cash, never redistributed. BTC/ETH/SOL each carry their own destination weight (2.0/1.5/0.5%), replacing the prior aggregate 10% crypto sleeve.
- Crypto sleeve: **ETH, SOL, BTC only.** HYPE removed from targets (July 2026 decision, consolidated 2026-07-12). BTC was the sleeve's designated liquid reserve; fully sold 2026-07-13 to trim the sleeve back toward target (see Open Items) — sleeve is now ETH/SOL only until BTC is rebuilt.

## Workflow (the only loop)
1. I deposit money and report buying power (or report available margin — check whether it's cash or margin before running the allocator; margin doesn't grow the book/target baseline the way cash does).
2. Positions are **live-priced automatically** (2026-07-13, extended to crypto 2026-07-14, extended to SKHY 2026-07-14): `holdings.yaml`'s `shares:` block (ticker → share count) and `crypto_shares:` block (coin → qty) are the source of truth for every normal position and coin — value = qty × latest Alpaca price, recomputed on every run, no manual resync needed for price moves. After a real buy/sell/trim changes a share count, paste `TICKER qty` lines → run `update-shares`; for a coin, `update-crypto-shares` (**merge mode; never `--replace` without my explicit confirmation**). BTC is manual until the reserve is rebuilt (no qty to track at $0) — paste screenshots or `TICKER value` lines → run `update-holdings`. Paste the margin screen too when it's changed → run `update-margin <debt> <buffer_pct>` (use Robinhood's own **displayed** buffer %, never derive it — see `allocate.py`'s `write_state()` comment for why).
3. Run `python allocate.py --cash X` for a cash deposit, or `--margin X` for margin-funded buying power (the two are gated differently — margin is clipped to the leverage cap and blocked below the buffer floor).
4. Present the recommendation table; I execute manually and confirm fills.
5. Sync share counts (and margin, if it changed) after execution.

**"Run a fresh allocation check" defaults to last-synced buying-power/margin state (2026-07-13).** Don't ask for a new buying-power/margin screenshot on every check — use the buying-power figure and margin state (debt/buffer) already on record in `holdings.yaml` unless I explicitly say something changed. I will proactively report deposits, withdrawals, margin draws/paydowns, or buffer changes when they happen; absent that, assume nothing moved. Portfolio value (gross/net equity) is computed fresh from all holdings on every run, **and since 2026-07-13 that includes live re-pricing** via `shares:` — no separate resync needed for ordinary price moves, only for a real share-count change (buy/sell/trim) or the BTC/margin manual paths above.

## Git sync — automatic, not a request
This repo is worked from multiple sessions (laptop, phone, browser). No session's local copy is authoritative — GitHub (`origin/main`) is. To keep every session honest without me having to say "push":
- **Start of every session**: `git pull` before reading `holdings.yaml`/`targets.yaml`/CLAUDE.md or doing anything — do this unprompted, first action, before touching state.
- **After any commit-worthy change** — classify the request first under `governance/decisions/OPS-0014-routine-operational-sync.md`. A read-only request (Class 0) makes no mutation, branch, or commit. A routine fact (Class 2 — exact share/crypto quantities, margin debt, a confirmed deposit/withdrawal/execution/non-execution) goes on its own narrow branch with the exact delta previewed first, and opens a draft PR under `OPS-0014`/`OPS-0009`'s standing procedure — it is never committed or pushed directly to `main`, and still requires my exact-head acceptance before merge (independent review may be waived only where `OPS-0009` §2's Lane M exemption applies). A material correction or any tier/target/gate/margin/allocator/policy change escalates to the fuller Class 3/4 lifecycle, with independent review required. No repository code ever places, routes, or submits a brokerage order. Don't wait to be asked to open that branch/PR — do it unprompted, and don't batch multiple unrelated changes into one commit — but never write directly to `main`.
- If `git pull` finds local changes not yet pushed from a prior session, stash-or-resolve and push those FIRST before proceeding — never silently overwrite unpushed state from another session.
- `.env` and anything git-ignored never gets committed — this rule is about tracked state only (YAML configs, CLAUDE.md, reports, logs).
- **After normal preflight, a substantial session reads `operations/WORKSTREAMS.yaml`** (the Portfolio-HQ workstream register — see `governance/decisions/OPS-0001-portfolio-hq-workstream-register.md`) and verifies its cached fields against live repository and GitHub state before acting on them. It never substitutes for this preflight or for live-state verification, and it's updated only after a material workstream change (status, milestone, blocker, authority, or next-action) — not on routine read-only checks.

Allocation logic (as implemented in `allocate.py`, migrated to the canonical destination architecture by PHQ-2026-02): fill **largest dollar gaps first**, $25 minimum lot, gated in order — **actionable gate** (`gates.yaml` — a gated name is never a buy candidate; its target capital simply isn't spent, never redistributed) → **RESERVE/CASH** (never a buy candidate, definitionally satisfied) → **200-SMA trend** (adds blocked unless RSI(14) < 30; skipped for crypto) → **7-day earnings blackout** (skipped for crypto) → **caps** (correlated-cluster caps, ≤ their own % of book each — see below, now binding on only their surviving canonical members) → **8% effective-issuer / 40% AI-platform common-driver no-add ceilings** (`issuer_lookthrough.yaml` — clip or block a buy, never a trim). Trims: a cluster-cap breach trims mechanically with **no RSI gate** (risk-limit breach, not a timing call) — largest-own-target-overweight-first, floored at each name's own destination target. **Retired by PHQ-2026-02**: the band/spec RSI-gated opportunistic trim and the T1/T2 1.5x concentration-ceiling trim (`gates.t1t2_trim_mult`) — both were defined in terms of tiers the canonical architecture doesn't have; no equivalent per-name trim rule was invented in their place. A ticker in more than one cluster needs room in all of them to buy. `--review` = no-cash rebalance check; `--levels` = buy-rung staging; `--health` = read-only portfolio risk-proximity snapshot showing margin state, leverage/buffer context, cluster-cap proximity, and 8%/40% no-add ceiling proximity. It adds no cash or margin, changes no allocator decision, writes no state, produces no composite health score, and places no orders.

**Correlated-cluster caps** (`targets.yaml` → `caps.clusters`, config truth): **semis** (ASML/TSM/NVDA/AVGO/AMD/MU/MRVL/KLAC/LRCX/AMAT/WDC/INTC/SKHY) ≤ 25% of book — chip names crash together, correlation-defined not market-cap-defined. **power_infra** (GEV/ETN/VRT/PWR) ≤ 20% of book, added 2026-07-14 — AI data-center power/electricity buildout theme, 0.560 avg pairwise correlation (trailing ~1yr) vs semis' 0.542 baseline. CAT and TSLA also correlated with this group in the same scan but were deliberately excluded — weaker fundamental fit, more likely bull-market beta than the specific mechanism (see Decisions Log). **oil** (XOM/CVX) ≤ 20% of book, added 2026-07-14 — same commodity driver, 0.819 avg pairwise correlation, the strongest found all session; only 3.46% of book when added, cap level derived from a stress test (see Decisions Log) not picked. **Membership unchanged by PHQ-2026-02** (not re-scoped — that's a separate future decision), but most prior members exited the canonical v1.30 roster in the transition, so each cap now binds on only its surviving canonical members: semis → ASML/TSM/NVDA/AVGO/KLAC; power_infra → ETN/GEV/PWR; oil → none remaining.

**T1/T2 concentration ceiling — retired 2026-07-31 (PHQ-2026-02).** Formerly: `targets.yaml` → `gates.t1t2_trim_mult`, default 1.5 — any T1 or T2 name above 1.5× its own tier target trimmed mechanically to target, no RSI gate. Defined entirely in terms of the T1/T2 tiers PHQ-2026-02's canonical-destination migration removed; no equivalent per-name trim rule was invented in its place — see the Decisions Log entry below for the historical record of why the rule existed.

## Decisions Log (do not relitigate without new evidence)
- **June 2026** — Band-overlay backtest: 227% vs **422% buy-and-hold** on the same basket → **NO-GO on automated trading**. Edge lives in the Phase 1 EMA base signal only; elaborate analysis layers were anti-predictive.
- **June 2026** — Stack & Rotate / 5-Point Filter / 3-tier TP system scrapped. Old BRK.B/UBER stops and TP ladders are dead.
- **July 2026** — Manual execution with decision support chosen over agentic trading.
- **July 2026** — Crypto = 10% target, ETH/SOL/BTC; HYPE out.
- **July 2026** — S/R levels (swing extrema, volume nodes, pivots) ruled computable and eligible for `--levels` rungs **only if a backtest beats the current SMA−ATR rungs**. Chart-pattern reading (flags, H&S, wave counts) permanently excluded — not computable, not backtestable.
- **July 2026** — **Crypto 10% reaffirmed as conviction-sizing, not a timing call.** The sleeve rebuilds through the gap machine only — it competes in the allocator's ranking like any underweight, with no timing gates (no trend/RSI/earnings; "cheap vs expensive" views don't size it). No lump-sum adds on directional views.
- **July 2026** — **Margin doctrine revised.** Discovered ~$4,423 in existing margin debt (~1.8x gross/equity) contradicting the prior "no margin" rule. Formally allowed going forward within a **fixed 1.8x structural leverage cap** and **30% buffer floor** (forced de-lever guardrail below that). Explicitly rejected: any margin-timing model ("borrow more when conditions look good") — same category as the band-overlay backtest and the multi-timeframe/Fable-5 research proposals floated the same session. Leverage amplifies whatever edge already exists (or doesn't); it is not itself a source of edge, and is not backtestable as one.
- **July 2026** — **Rung backtest verdict: NO CHANGE** (`reports/rung_backtest.md`). Three arms, 2021–2026, 65 tickers, pre-committed 1.0pp threshold: market-buy 30.12% > current rungs 29.35% > S/R rungs 29.05% annualized TWR. S/R rungs REJECTED (lost to both). C−A gap +0.77pp — rung-waiting cost money but inside noise, so `--levels` survives as staging aid. **Question closed; no variants, no re-runs without a new regime in the data.**
- **July 2026** — **AMZN kept at T2 (not promoted to T1).** At the time of that decision, T1 was 7-of-9 names in the identified AI-infrastructure grouping (ASML/TSM/NVDA/MSFT/GOOGL/META/GEV), which had been flagged as a concentration risk in a system deep-critique the same session. Sizing up another mega-cap while that finding sat unaddressed would have compounded the risk rather than fixing it. COST was later promoted to T1 under TGT-0002 as a distinct consumer-membership driver; AMZN remains T2 under its existing unsuperseded reasoning.
- **July 2026** — **Regime-gate backtest verdict: DROP THE GATE** (`reports/regime_backtest.md`). Two arms, 2021–2026, 63 T1-spec tickers (no crypto), pre-committed 1.0pp threshold, weight scheme and trim rule held at current production values so only the QQQ 200-EMA gate varied: current (gate ON, held 15 of 62 deposits as cash) 25.49% vs no-gate 28.05% — a 2.56pp/yr gap, more than double the threshold. **Removed**: `allocate.py`'s `render()` no longer blocks/zeroes deployment when QQQ is below its 200-day EMA; deposits always invest immediately based on gaps. Regime status is still computed and shown in every check (informational only, e.g. "ABOVE 200-EMA (risk-on)") — just no longer gates anything. Unlike the trim decision, no leverage-based counter-argument applies here: deploying a cash deposit during a downturn doesn't increase leverage (debt is fixed, both gross and equity grow by the deposit amount, so leverage ticks down slightly, not up) — the concrete danger that overrode the trim backtest's raw result doesn't have an analog for this one. Two honest caveats on record: this window (2022) contains one real but not prolonged/severe bear stretch, the gate's actual test case; and a behavioral case (forced pause during a confirmed downtrend reduces panic-sell risk) is real but not something a TWR backtest can price. **Question closed under the same pre-committed discipline as trims/rungs/weights; no variants, no re-runs without a new regime in the data.**
- **July 2026** — **Trend-gate backtest verdict: NO CHANGE** (`reports/trend_backtest.md`). Two arms, 2021–2026, 63 T1-spec tickers (no crypto), pre-committed 1.0pp threshold, weights/trims at production values, no regime gate in either arm (dropped from production before this test): current (gate ON, 930 buy attempts blocked/redirected) 27.64% vs no-gate 28.05% — a +0.41pp gap for no-gate, inside noise. This closed the system audit: it was the last untested gate in the buy path. Key contrast with the regime gate (dropped the same day at 2.56pp): a regime-gate block held whole deposits as *cash* (pure drag), while a trend-gate block only *redirects* money to the next-ranked gap, still fully invested — which is exactly why its measured cost is ~6x smaller and lands inside noise. The earnings blackout remains untested and is accepted as **untestable** (historical earnings dates for 63 tickers over 5 years aren't available on the free feed); its mechanism is the same money-redirect as the trend gate, so its cost is bounded by the same logic. **Audit complete — every mechanical rule in the buy path now tested exactly once; question closed; no variants, no re-runs without a new regime in the data.**
- **July 2026** — **Weight backtest verdict: NO CHANGE** (`reports/weight_backtest.md`). Four arms, 2021–2026, 63 T1-spec tickers (no crypto), pre-committed 1.0pp threshold, band/RSI trim rule held constant so only tier weighting varied: current (28.05%) vs equal-weight (28.41%), flatter gradation (27.83%), steeper gradation (28.80%) — all within 0.75pp, noise. The tier-weight *structure* has no measurable return edge over this window in either direction; weighting exists to express conviction, not to chase performance, and this test confirms there wasn't a performance difference being left on the table. **Question closed; no variants, no re-runs without a new regime in the data.**
- **July 2026** — **Third concentration cap added: oil (XOM/CVX), 20% of book — cap level derived, not picked.** Same-commodity mechanism (crude oil price drives both almost entirely) produced the strongest correlation of any group scanned all session: 0.819 avg pairwise, vs semis' 0.542 and power_infra's 0.560. Only 3.46% of book when added — nowhere near binding on size alone, so instead of picking a round number the level was derived via stress test: this dataset's own actual historical max drawdown for XOM/CVX (~30%, cached 2020–2025 window, not a real-world citation) applied at the cap ceiling produces ~6% of book max impact, matching power_infra's already-accepted risk budget (20% cap × 30% crash = 6%; semis' looser 25% cap implies 7.5%, justified by 13-name internal diversification vs oil's 2). Landed at the same 20% as power_infra from two offsetting effects: oil's correlation is higher and longer-established (argues looser) but only 2 names means less internal diversification against a company-specific shock (argues tighter). `targets.yaml`'s `caps.clusters` extended to a third entry — no code changes needed, the generalized cluster-cap mechanism built for power_infra handles this natively.
- **July 2026** — **Tier-fit scan: AAPL promoted band → T2; AVGO stays at T2 (not promoted to T1).** A one-time structural scan (objective company scale/sector facts, not fundamental research or return prediction — see the declined "rationalize all 65 holdings" request the same session) found two roster placements worth a real look, same lens as AMZN. **AVGO**: already a semis-cluster member: promoting it to T1 would compound the flagged T1 AI-infra concentration more directly than AMZN would have, since AVGO is itself semis-correlated — kept at T2, same verdict and reasoning as AMZN. **AAPL**: promoted band → T2. Unlike AVGO, this doesn't touch the flagged T1 concentration (AAPL isn't moving to T1), and T2 stays diversified either way (Info Tech goes from 1-of-14 to 2-of-15, not a new single-theme problem). AAPL's profile — scale, stability, blue-chip dominance — fit T2's then-existing "quality compounder" character (alongside COST/WMT/MA/BRK.B at the time; COST was later promoted to T1 under TGT-0002) better than band's mixed cyclical/smaller-cap company. Rest of the 65-name roster scanned and found to be reasonable fits for their tiers; no other placements flagged.
- **July 2026** — **VMC consolidated into MLM, VMC exited.** Both are direct aggregates-industry peers (crushed stone/sand/gravel; same infrastructure-spending/housing/rate-cycle drivers) — holding both wasn't real diversification, just redundant sector exposure. MLM (T2, 1.65%) carries the exposure alone going forward.
- **July 2026** — **Trim backtest verdict: KEEP 1.25x / RSI 60, no amendments** (`reports/trim_backtest.md`). Five arms, 2021–2026, 37 band/spec tickers, pre-committed 1.0pp threshold. Parameter variants (1.15x/55, 1.40x/65, mechanical no-RSI-gate) all within ±0.26pp of current → noise; nothing to re-tune; per-ticker tailored parameters rejected as overfitting. Never-trim (control) won the window by +6.65pp/yr — recorded honestly as the pre-committed "trims are drag" outcome — but did it by letting RKLB (spec tier) balloon to 12.6% of the sleeve (~3.6x intended size) with -28.6% vs -24.6% MaxDD. Under the account's ~1.6x leverage that is ~-46% vs ~-39% on net equity — deep enough to threaten the 30% buffer floor and force selling at the bottom, converting temporary drawdown into permanent loss; the unlevered backtest got to hold through its worst moment, a levered account may not. Trims are the enforcement mechanism of tier sizing (risk control), not a return layer; the 6.65pp is the insurance premium in a bull-only sample. **Question closed; no variants, no re-runs without a new regime in the data.**
- **July 2026** — **Second concentration cap added: power_infra (GEV/ETN/VRT/PWR), 20% of book.** Full-book sector audit found three single-name "sectors" (Materials/MLM, Utilities/CEG, Real Estate/EQIX, each <2.2% of book — thin but immaterial) and zero fixed-income exposure (correct given ~5% margin cost, not a gap to fill — GLD does the ballast job bonds would). No new tier warranted; the existing six (T1/T2/ETF/band/spec/crypto) already span the full conviction spectrum. Separately, a one-time correlation scan (trailing ~1yr daily returns, pairwise Pearson, same rigor as the trim/rung backtests) found GEV/ETN/VRT/PWR/CAT/TSLA correlated at 0.560 avg — comparable to the semis cluster's own 0.542 baseline (which is capped). Built the same mechanical no-RSI-gate cap for GEV/ETN/VRT/PWR only (real shared driver: AI data-center power/electricity buildout demand). CAT and TSLA were deliberately excluded from the cap despite testing correlated — weaker fundamental fit (general industrials; EV/energy storage), more likely riding bull-market beta than the specific power-buildout mechanism, and including weak-fit names would dilute the cap's meaning the way the semis cap's own comment warns against ("measured honestly so the cap actually binds"). Cap set at 20% (tighter than semis' 25%, reflecting a shorter track record and narrower evidence base — one bull-market-year sample) with room above the 10.5% level the 4-name group sat at when added, so it doesn't fire off a diagnostic finding alone. `allocate.py`'s `plan()` generalized from a single hardcoded semis cluster to a `caps.clusters` list — any ticker in more than one cluster needs room in all of them to buy; each cluster's mechanical trim runs independently, largest-own-target-overweight-first. Declined a broader "track correlation across everything continuously" system in the same conversation — same rejected pattern as every other standing-analysis-layer proposal; this cap is a bounded, one-time, actionable finding, not an ongoing signal. Also from the same scan: the enterprise-software group (CRM/MSFT/NOW, 0.650 correlation) was considered and declined — fails both the mechanism test (generic "software sector" beta, not a narrow specific driver like power-buildout) and the size test (6.8% of book, but 4.8% of that is just MSFT's existing T1 position; ex-MSFT weight is ~2%, immaterial). Cluster caps are also confirmed **ceiling-only by design, no target/floor** — a ticker's own tier weight already carries its conviction sizing; a cluster target would mean buying toward a thematic exposure goal, which is a different (and here, wrong-direction) decision than "these move together, cap the downside risk," especially with T1 already flagged as AI-infrastructure-concentrated.
- **July 2026** — **T1 AI-infra cluster cap: scanned and declined.** An external doctrine review correctly flagged that T1's long-standing AI-infrastructure concentration (7-of-9 names: ASML/TSM/NVDA/MSFT/GOOGL/META/GEV, previously only ever cited as a reason to *decline* new adds like AMZN/AVGO, never addressed directly) had never actually been correlation-scanned like semis/power_infra/oil. Ran the same one-time scan (trailing ~1yr daily returns, pairwise Pearson): **avg 0.302 (all 7), 0.373 excluding MSFT** — well below every existing cap (semis 0.542, power_infra 0.560, oil 0.819) and below the 0.650 enterprise-software group that was already declined for weak mechanism fit. This cluster is the same category, one level more diffuse: rate-expectations/index-flow beta across mega-cap tech, not a narrow shared driver like power-buildout demand or crude oil price. **Declined on the same bar as CRM/MSFT/NOW — no cap added.** Live check at scan time: T1 was 42.1% of book vs its 30.15% target (9 × 3.35%), with the 7-name AI-infra subset alone at 31.2% — confirming the concentration is real, just not correlation-cap-shaped. **Root cause identified instead:** T1 and T2 have no trim rule at all — only `band`/`spec` tiers trim on overweight in `plan()` — so T1 positions compound past target with nothing mechanical pulling them back. That's a bigger doctrine question (a new T1/T2 trim rule) requiring the same backtest discipline as every other rule here (see `trim_backtest.md` precedent) — flagged, not implemented same-session. **Question closed on the cluster-cap approach; no re-scan without new evidence.** GEV's multi-cluster membership (would have been the first case, alongside power_infra) stayed hypothetical — GEV remains in exactly one cluster.
- **July 2026** — **T1/T2 trim backtest verdict: NO CHANGE, no trim rule adopted** (`reports/t1t2_trim_backtest.md`). Direct follow-up to the T1 AI-infra cluster-cap decline above — the cluster-cap approach failed on correlation, but the underlying drift (T1 42.1% of book vs 30.15% target, nothing mechanical correcting it) was real, so this test asked the question directly: should a core-conviction T1/T2 position's overweight ever get capped, separate from any cluster question. New test, not a rerun of `trim_backtest.md` (that one covered band/spec, RSI-gated, opportunistic trims on names that ran hot — a different failure mode than capping a conviction position mid-drawdown). Four arms designed, 63-ticker T1-spec universe (no crypto, 24 in T1/T2), weight scheme/band-spec trim rule/trend gate held at production values, pre-committed threshold 1.0pp **plus** a MaxDD tolerance of 1.0pp (a TWR win bought with a materially deeper drawdown doesn't count — direct application of the `trim_backtest.md` precedent, where never-trim won on raw TWR but was rejected on leverage/forced-selling grounds): A — current (no T1/T2 trim) 27.64%, -20.3% MaxDD; B — mechanical 1.5x threshold, no RSI gate, 27.66%, -20.1% MaxDD; C — same 1.5x but skipped while the name is below its own 200-SMA, 27.66%, -20.1% MaxDD (**identical to B** — a name over 1.5x of target is, in this window, never simultaneously in a confirmed downtrend, so the two arms never diverge; a real, sensible result, not a bug). Arm D (AI-infra cluster-relative cap) was pre-committed to run only if B and C diverged by >1.0pp; they didn't (0.00pp gap), so D never ran — exactly the conditional discipline it was designed under. Gaps vs A: B/C +0.02pp TWR, MaxDD *improves* by 0.15pp — both trivially inside the noise threshold. **Verdict scope, precisely: this closes the *return* case for a T1/T2 trim rule, not the *capital-impairment* case** — same distinction the regime-gate entry drew between "cash drag, fully priced by TWR" and "capital impairment, not priced by TWR." The aggregate MaxDD numbers above are portfolio-level, the same quantity that was *not* where `trim_backtest.md`'s override case lived (that one was built on RKLB's own single-name drawdown, not the portfolio's). Ran the same single-name decomposition here: under Arm A's no-trim control, **NVDA peaked at 2.14x its T1 target with its own real -66.4% peak-to-trough (its actual 2021-2022 correction)** — at the account's current 1.44x leverage, that's severe enough that the linear drawdown×leverage translation (the same one used for RKLB) breaks down as a literal number; the honest read is that NVDA at that size sits in forced-liquidation/buffer-floor-breach territory on its own historical worst case, not a diversified-portfolio-level event. **The no-change verdict stands for the return question only. Current (no T1/T2 trim) stays in production; whether T1's real-world 42.1% concentration deserves a trim on capital-impairment grounds is a separate, still-open judgment this backtest surfaces the numbers for but does not itself settle** — flagged as its own Open Item rather than folded into this closed question. Sample-size caveat carried over from every prior backtest: one window, one mostly-rising sample with a single real bear stretch (2022) — this window's NVDA drawdown IS that stretch, not a hypothetical. **The return question is closed; no re-runs without a new regime in the data. The capital-impairment question is not closed and isn't a backtest question — see Open Items.**
- **July 2026** — **Semis cluster cap: leverage asymmetry identified, denominator left as net equity (book), no code change.** Margin debt is fixed while gross assets move, so the cap's book denominator shrinks *faster* than assets in a broad decline (can nudge the cluster over cap with zero real dispersion — no semis-specific move needed) and grows faster than assets in a broad rally (quietly loosens the cap even while semis itself gains). Verified against live numbers both directions. Real relative outperformance (semis specifically rallying vs. the rest of the book) still correctly triggers the cap **and** correctly grows every other position's target from the resulting bigger book — that rebalancing behavior (sell the concentrated winner, fund the underweight laggards) is sound and left untouched. Switching the cap's denominator to gross would remove the decline-side asymmetry but would understate true leverage-adjusted concentration risk during rallies, which is worse. Documented as a known structural property of a net-equity-denominated risk cap under leverage, not a bug to fix.
- **July 2026** — **Portfolio Intelligence Engine Version 2 frozen as canonical specification** (`docs/PORTFOLIO_INTELLIGENCE_SPEC.md`; structured record `decision_log.yaml` PI-0001). A future, optional layer for per-company thesis/risk/catalyst/conviction/review records, sitting **above** the allocator, never coupled to it: **advisory-only**, cannot modify `holdings.yaml`/`targets.yaml`/`allocate.py`, cannot alter any allocator recommendation, and no production code reads it under this specification. **The filesystem is the index** — an explicit `intelligence/index.yaml` was evaluated on seven criteria (single source of truth, git merge behavior, discoverability, reporting, validation, scalability, implementation complexity) and rejected on all seven; this is frozen doctrine, reconsiderable only through a new documented architectural decision backed by materially changed evidence, same standard as every other frozen call in this log. Each covered company gets **at most one structured YAML and one thesis Markdown file** — risks, catalysts, conviction rating, review cadence, and review history all live inside the company YAML, not scattered across topic-keyed files. `portfolio_role_ref` stores only the tier label, never a numeric weight — **`targets.yaml` remains sole authority for tier weights and allocation policy**, full stop. Coverage is opt-in; a missing company file is never an error. **Freezing this specification authorizes nothing beyond the document itself** — no company files, validators, reports, or allocator integrations exist yet, and any future allocator-visible integration (even a display-only footnote) requires its own separate research, justification, testing, and approval, the same bar every existing gate in `allocate.py` was held to.
- **July 2026** — **Portfolio Intelligence Phase 1 implementation authorized, bounded** (`decision_log.yaml` PI-0002). An implementation-readiness review returned REVISE on the first Phase 1 proposal solely for lacking this authorization — PI-0001 froze the design but explicitly didn't authorize building anything. PI-0002 authorizes exactly one thing: a standalone, read-only `intelligence_validator.py` (validating synthetic-only company YAML against the schema already frozen in the spec's §9/14/16/20/21) plus its own isolated test file. Missing/empty `intelligence/` coverage must return success with zero companies — absence stays a non-error. **No real company file, thesis content, or template is created by this authorization** — the spec's own suggested "one hand-authored file first" step is a human editorial act, not implementation work, so it's deliberately excluded here. Symbol resolution is also deferred — moot at this scope since no real ticker filenames exist yet to resolve. Validator must have zero import coupling with `allocate.py`/`margin_state.py` in either direction and must never write anything. **This is a bounded experiment, not authorization for the broader Intelligence subsystem** — PI-0001 remains the governing architectural decision, the implementation lands on its own separate branch/PR, this governance entry does not itself authorize merging that future PR, and any scope expansion needs its own new documented decision.
- **July 2026** — **Portfolio Intelligence Phase 2 pilot authorized, bounded** (`decision_log.yaml` PI-0003). A same-session readiness review found the Phase 1 validator faithful to the frozen spec with no blocking defects and confirmed a single human-authored company is the narrowest reversible next step in the spec's own incremental sequence. **One company beats every alternative considered**: a template file doesn't fit the spec's ticker-only directory model (same reasoning PI-0002 already applied); more synthetic examples add nothing Phase 1's 28 tests didn't already cover, since the whole point of a first real file is testing the schema against actual human judgment; **multiple companies are intentionally deferred** — same smallest-reversible-step discipline as every other gate in this log (rungs, trims, weights, the T1/T2 ceiling), letting the schema and workflow get reviewed before any roster-wide pattern is set; and reporting infrastructure comes strictly after real content exists to report on, not before. **Allocator integration stays out of scope for the same reason it always has** — any allocator-visible integration is gated behind its own separate research/justification/testing/approval, and this pilot doesn't attempt that bar; it's a content experiment, not an integration one. PI-0003 authorizes exactly: `intelligence/companies/`, one real `TICKER.yaml`, one matching `TICKER.md`, human-authored only, validated read-only via the existing validator — **no second company, no allocator integration, no generated conviction score, no symbol resolution, no dashboards/reports/rollups/`index.yaml`, no `holdings.yaml`/`targets.yaml` change**. `portfolio_role_ref` doctrine is unchanged from the frozen spec, refined for separation of concerns, not broadened: it is **descriptive only** — a human's own characterization of a company's role, fixed at authoring time, that does not authorize or derive any allocation behavior. Its meaning does not float with whatever `targets.yaml` currently contains — **changes to allocator implementation (sleeves, bands, tiers, weights, any allocation mechanics) never retroactively change the meaning of an existing Portfolio Intelligence record.** This decision deliberately does **not** enumerate a specific role list in Portfolio Intelligence governance text, since hardcoding one would smuggle an allocator-internal implementation detail into a layer that's supposed to stay independent of it; if role semantics ever need to change, that's its own future, separately-documented governance decision, never silently inherited from an allocator-side change. **This remains a reversible architectural checkpoint** — the pilot lands on its own branch/PR, gets reviewed before any second company is considered, and can be discarded or revised without touching anything outside `intelligence/`, since nothing else reads it.
- **July 2026** — **Portfolio Intelligence Phase 2 pilot review closed; expansion conditionally authorized** (`decision_log.yaml` PI-0004). COST (PR #71) implemented and merged, validator and full test suite passing, followed by an architecture retrospective — but PI-0003 left "reviewed" undefined as a completion criterion, so the pilot gate wasn't actually closeable until this decision. **Defines "pilot reviewed"** as a reusable five-part completion criterion: implementation merged to main, validator passing, full test suite passing, an architectural retrospective completed, and any governance ambiguities the retrospective surfaces explicitly resolved by governance — the fifth condition is satisfied by this very decision. **Freezes `conviction.rating`** to a closed four-value vocabulary — `Low`, `Medium`, `High`, `Very High`, nothing else, no numeric scale, no hyphenated hybrid — required now (not deferred) because it's one of the schema's few explicitly human-judgment fields and the ambiguity was demonstrated, not hypothetical: COST's own drafting hit this directly (a proposed "Medium-High" value fit neither of the frozen spec's two illustrative example scales). COST's existing `High` rating already conforms — no migration needed. **Deliberately leaves `risk.severity`/`risk.status` open** — one company record is insufficient evidence to justify a closed vocabulary for either field, unlike conviction; revisit once 3-5 records exist and real usage patterns are observable, same anti-overfitting discipline as `trim_backtest.md`'s rejection of per-ticker tailored parameters. **Note on enforcement:** the conviction vocabulary is closed as doctrine, not as code — `intelligence_validator.py` is unchanged by this decision and does not yet enforce it; that would be its own future, separately-authorized validator change. **This decision authorizes nothing beyond itself** — no second company, no schema change, no validator change; it closes the PI-0003 review gate and states that a future expansion phase (a second company, or a validator enforcement change) may now proceed through its own separate authorization, preserving the same Governance → Implementation → Validation → Retrospective → Governance cadence this log has followed throughout.
- **July 2026** — **Second Portfolio Intelligence company authorized: XOM** (`decision_log.yaml` PI-0005). A same-session, read-only candidate review screened all 61 eligible current holdings on architecture/workflow suitability (not investment attractiveness) and recommended XOM — its screening score isn't restated here as a company attribute, only the completed review is referenced. **XOM is the only company PI-0005 authorizes** — exactly the future creation of `intelligence/companies/XOM.yaml` and `intelligence/companies/XOM.md`, nothing else; adopting this decision does not itself create those files. XOM adds real novelty beyond COST without touching schema/validator/vocabulary: a different `portfolio_role_ref` (`band`, the corpus's first non-T2 value, still descriptive-only per PI-0003's unchanged doctrine), commodity/cyclical risk framing distinct from COST's structural/operational profile, a different catalyst shape (capex/dividend cycles vs. expansion guidance), and a multi-segment business that stays reviewable without forcing schema complexity — unlike bank or holding-company candidates the same review screened out. **Requires the same human approvals COST required** — `portfolio_role_ref`, `conviction.rating` (must be exactly one of PI-0004's four frozen values, never computed/scored/inferred), conviction rationale, review cadence, final thesis/risks/catalysts, and source-access disclosure — before any implementation PR opens. **`risk.severity`/`risk.status` stay exactly as unfrozen as PI-0004 left them** — two records still doesn't meet PI-0004's own 3-5 revisit trigger, so this decision doesn't advance that question. **No schema, validator, allocator, `targets.yaml`/`holdings.yaml`/margin, reporting, or `COST.yaml`/`COST.md` change is authorized, and no third company is authorized** — a successfully implemented XOM record does not itself authorize further expansion; that needs its own future decision, same pattern as every prior phase in this log. Implementation requires its own separate branch/PR and its own separate final pre-merge review before merge, same discipline as PR #71 and PR #72.
- **July 2026** — **Theme Intelligence data model frozen (`decision_log.yaml` PI-0006), no implementation authorized.** Follows from PI-0005, precisely stated: PI-0005 authorized a second Company Intelligence record (XOM) to test generalization beyond COST, but that implementation is not merged to `main` — `intelligence/companies/` currently holds only `COST.yaml`/`COST.md`. This freeze is grounded on what's actually merged: one working company record validating cleanly, isolation tests passing, and PI-0004's conviction vocabulary now enforced in code. It does not claim the workflow is proven across two companies. Freezes the flat data model: one `theme_id`, two files per theme, one-way authority only (company → theme, never the reverse), no stored sibling index or graph — "the graph is a reporting view, not a stored data model," the same filesystem-as-index discipline PI-0001 applied to Portfolio Intelligence. Themes carry evidence/risks/catalysts and a closed `lifecycle` vocabulary (`Emerging/Established/Mature/Declining/Archived`) but **no conviction rating and no numeric score**. Roadmap position: Theme Intelligence precedes Portfolio Intelligence aggregation, still fully deferred. **This is a freeze only** and does not itself authorize or permit any implementation. See the companion PI-0007 entry, which separately authorizes the one pilot slice conforming to this freeze.
- **July 2026** — **Theme Intelligence bounded pilot authorized (`decision_log.yaml` PI-0007).** Follows from PI-0006's freeze. Authorizes exactly one theme, `ai_infrastructure`, referenced by two new company records, **NVDA and GEV** — both already T1 holdings in `targets.yaml`. CLAUDE.md's own "T1 AI-infra cluster cap: scanned and declined" entry directly names both as part of the same 7-name AI-infrastructure subset it scanned (31.2% of book, correlation 0.302, cap declined) and states that concentration was "previously only ever cited as a reason to decline new adds... never addressed directly." That no record anywhere documents the *shared reasoning* behind the concentration is this decision's own inference from the current absence of any Theme Intelligence record — not a claim the cited entry makes itself. The pair also crosses two real correlation clusters — NVDA in `targets.yaml`'s `semis`, GEV in its `power_infra` — deliberately, testing whether cluster and theme membership coexist without collision. **Eight files total, two categories**: six intelligence content files plus two implementation files (`intelligence_validator.py`, `test_intelligence_validator.py`), limited to the minimum schema-enforcement change. **No change to `docs/PORTFOLIO_INTELLIGENCE_SPEC.md` is authorized, including no new section.** Regression requirement is merge-order-independent and does not depend on XOM having merged (it has not as of this decision): COST.yaml must remain valid and unmodified; if XOM.yaml exists on `main` when implementation begins, it must also remain valid and unmodified. Explicitly prohibited: a second theme, a third company, Portfolio Intelligence aggregation, any allocator/targets/holdings/margin touch, any change to COST/XOM, any stored index. **Authorizes future implementation only.**
- **July 2026** — **Theme Intelligence validator hardening retroactively recorded (`decision_log.yaml` PI-0008).** PR #79 (commit `205bb3c`) made `lifecycle` and `review` required fields on every theme record (previously only checked when present) and added a check that a theme's declared `theme_id` matches its own filename — both already merged before this entry was written. Validator-only, backward-compatible with the existing `ai_infrastructure` theme. **Closes a governance-recording gap, not a new authorization.**

- **July 2026** — **Margin risk-state classifier (`margin_state.py`) retroactively recorded (`decision_log.yaml` MARGIN-0003).** `classify_margin_state()`/`concentration_risk_score()` run on every `allocate.py` invocation other than `--levels`/`--performance` and are displayed in `render()`'s Margin section (a "Margin risk state: NORMAL/CAUTION/RESTRICTED/FORCED_DELEVER" line) whenever margin debt or a margin request is present — confirmed live in production, not proposed. **This entry records existing behavior; it does not authorize anything new.** The classifier runs strictly after `plan()` returns and cannot alter a buy/trim/block decision — verified by tracing the call site. In practice only `NORMAL` and `FORCED_DELEVER` are reachable today: `targets.yaml`'s `margin:` block defines no `states:`/`concentration_adjustment:` config, so every `CAUTION`/`RESTRICTED` threshold parameter is unset and those escalation paths never fire; `FORCED_DELEVER` continues to come from the pre-existing, unmodified `margin_capacity()` hard check. No prior Decisions Log or `decision_log.yaml` entry existed for this module — a repository-wide planning review (2026-07-18) found the gap and this entry closes it. **The repository's root commit introduced this module, its live call site, and four Phase 2 planning docs describing it as an unimplemented, unapproved proposal, all in the same commit** — no reconstructable history establishes an approval sequence, and none is claimed here. Corrected separately: `docs/PHASE2_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`, `docs/PHASE2B_REVIEW.md`, `docs/PHASE2D_INTEGRATION_PLAN.md`, `docs/MARGIN_INTELLIGENCE_DESIGN.md` (see status corrections). **This decision records the current implementation state. Future enhancements to the Margin Intelligence Engine — threshold calibration, `targets.yaml` state values, and repayment recommendations — remain subject to their own governance and are outside the scope of this entry.**

All seven initial enrollment rows (COST/PI-0003, XOM/PI-0005, NVDA+GEV/PI-0007, ISRG+TMO/PI-0009, TSM/PI-0012) start `monitoring_enabled: false`; all seven checkpoints start `checkpoint_status: pending` with no baseline filing value asserted. `PI-0014` (INTC/SYK/DHR) is identified as the framework's intended first Path-B pilot, but `PI-0014`'s own accepted scope remains exactly what it already was — one bounded, conversation-based, read-only exercise with conversational findings and no repository artifact; this decision does not convert it to unattended execution or GitHub issue output, and does not itself authorize any GitHub Actions workflow, executable code, live adapter endpoint, or runtime prompt/schema — persistent issue output, unattended execution, scheduling, and activation for `PI-0014` (or any Path-B task) each require their own later, separate implementation or activation authorization. Full rationale and complete mechanical specification in the linked decision record and `docs/FRESHNESS_PLANNER_V1_SPEC.md`, not restated here.
- **July 2026** — **AUTO-0002 accepted: local-only validation/evaluator/identity foundation authorized for the Research Freshness Framework** (`governance/decisions/AUTO-0002-freshness-local-foundation.md`). Authorizes a later, separate implementation PR only — the governance filing itself implements no code — for exactly three pure, deterministic, non-operational modules (`freshness_validator.py`, `freshness_state.py`, `freshness_identity.py`) plus their three corresponding test files, implementing `AUTO-0001`'s registry/checkpoint schema, §9 precedence rule, and a canonical-JSON/SHA-256 identity contract for the cadence fingerprint and task-instance ID. Filing-derived fingerprint computation and replacement-authorization validation are both deferred — `AUTO-0001` doesn't fix enough of either to implement without inventing new content. Grants no network, GitHub, Claude-invocation, monitoring-activation, allocator, margin, or `PI-0014` authority. Full rationale in the linked decision record, not restated here.
- **July 2026** — **AUTO-0003 accepted: Cadence Trigger Core (Local, Deterministic)** (`governance/decisions/AUTO-0003-cadence-trigger-core.md`). Governance filing only — the filing itself adds no code. Authorizes a later, separate implementation PR containing exactly `freshness_cadence.py` plus `test_freshness_cadence.py`, implementing one pure function (`compute_due_cadence_fingerprint`) that gates on `monitoring_enabled`/`checkpoint_status` and, only when a ticker is enabled, verified, and overdue, delegates to the existing `freshness_identity.compute_cadence_fingerprint`. Resolves `AUTO-0001`'s open `PI-0011` strict-overdue-rule (`review.next_due < as_of_date`) reuse-or-equivalence obligation through the equivalence path — `freshness_cadence.py` does not import or modify `intelligence_report.py`; `test_freshness_cadence.py` proves strict overdue-boundary equivalence, not whole-function equivalence, against its real `collect_staleness_findings` for schema-valid synthetic records through a shared parametrized fixture. No operational monitoring, adapter, scheduling, GitHub, or Claude-invocation authority is granted by this filing.
- **July 2026** — **TGT-0001 accepted: Additive Target-Budget Policy for Roster and Tier Changes** (`governance/decisions/TGT-0001-additive-target-budget-policy.md`). Future tier/roster changes (promote, demote, add, remove) are additive by default — no universal offsetting demotion required — but every change stays individually authorized, and several proposed changes must be assessed cumulatively. Every future proposal must disclose before/after nominal target totals, implied fully-filled gross-exposure multiples, and cluster-target totals; a new ex-ante governance rule blocks any proposal that would push a cluster's configured target total above its existing cap, and overall leverage capacity never overrides that rule. No separate nominal-target ceiling was adopted, and that absence is not permission for automatic or unreviewed cumulative expansion. A promotion never requests, authorizes, or recommends margin — target configuration and margin capacity/usage remain distinct concepts. Future proposals changing AMZN, AVGO, or AAPL must identify and explicitly supersede the applicable prior reasoning from PR #18 or PR #58; TGT-0001 does not itself change or supersede those current placements. No allocator, `targets.yaml`, margin, cluster-cap, trim, or crypto behavior changed.
- **July 2026** — **COST promoted T2 → T1** (`governance/decisions/TGT-0002-cost-promotion-t2-to-t1.md`). The principal determined that COST independently possesses the qualities necessary for T1 placement. The decision does not re-rank or reconsider existing T1 holdings, adopt a general T1 admission doctrine, or authorize another promotion. Filed under TGT-0001; no configured cluster is affected, and no margin, trade, or live allocation action is authorized.
- **July 2026** — **GOV-0003 accepted: margin-timing principle narrowed, conditional research permitted, hard limits unchanged** (`governance/decisions/GOV-0003-margin-conditional-research-permitted.md`). Constitution §3's absolute "no timing, ever, full stop" claim is narrowed: discretionary/predictive margin timing remains permanently excluded, but whether a rules-based, pre-registered, evidence-gated conditional deployment or repayment rule outperforms the current fixed posture is now an open empirical question, researchable only under its own separate charter and adoptable only through its own separate governance decision. Constitution §4 gains a narrow carve-out for exactly this kind of bounded, retrospective backtest — no standing predictive layer, opportunity map, price target, opaque score, continuous signal, or precomputed recommendation is authorized. **The 1.8x leverage cap and 30% buffer floor are unchanged**; any future conditional rule operates strictly inside them; a high buffer or low maintenance ratio is never automatic permission to borrow. Operator discretion is asymmetric: always free to decline, use less, or repay more/sooner; never free to exceed a governed limit or bypass a hard safeguard. Authorizes no research charter, no code, no allocator change, and no resolution of the future revised MARGIN-0004 workstream by itself.
- **July 2026** — **MARGIN-0004 accepted: Phase 3–7A margin research closure reconciled against GOV-0003** (`governance/decisions/MARGIN-0004-phase3-7a-closure-reconciliation.md`). The 2026-07-13 "Margin doctrine revised" entry above remains valid and unedited for what it actually rejected — discretionary or predictive margin-timing — that rejection still stands. What's new: GOV-0003 later permitted bounded, pre-registered, evidence-gated conditional-rule *research* (not adoption) as a distinct, narrower question that entry never addressed. This decision formally reconciles the already-closed Phase 3–7A margin research program's closure (`docs/PHASE7_RESEARCH_PROGRAM_CLOSURE.md`) against that narrowing — recording what Phase 3–7A tested, what it deliberately left untested (any conditional/timing-based deployment or repayment rule was outside its charter, not an oversight), and correcting two stale passages in `docs/MARGIN_DOCTRINE.md` that had promised a dated update once evidence existed, when that evidence has existed since Phase 3–7A closed. Authorizes no new research charter, no shared data model, no code, no simulation run, and no production behavior — the 1.8x cap and 30% floor are unchanged.

reports, thesis-break indicators) — explicitly no universal cadence. §13 records future scan design preferences (event-driven, low-maintenance, primary-source-preferring, collection, SEC/Federal Reserve/broker/news/market-data integration, generated research update, or production workflow coupling is authorized by this decision — any such implementation requires its own later, separate governance decision and bounded implementation PR. **Fourth same-day pre-merge, pre-review amendment (2026-07-25, PR #150 still zero GitHub reviews):** adds §16 (four subsections), reaches `complete` only after every authorized deliverable exists, its PR merges, tests/validators pass, and `operations/WORKSTREAMS.yaml`/`governance/decisions.yaml` are synchronized — never from - **July 2026** — **OPS-0007 accepted: capability-based independent-review standard, and a bounded provisional allocation-readiness bridge for WS-0005** (`governance/decisions/OPS-0007-capability-based-review-provisional-allocation-bridge.md`). Governance-only — creates no Company Intelligence record, comparison artifact, target scenario, or allocation check, and does not itself review, correct, mark ready, or merge PR #161. Replaces, for WS-0005 review gates from this decision's own merge forward, a reviewer requirement based solely on a named model ("Fable") with a twelve-point capability-based standard (independence from authorship, exact-head anchoring, sufficient repository/controlling-authority access, sufficient web/research capability, primary-source-inspection attempts, fact/inference/evidence-type separation, validator/test verification, severity classification, retained GitHub-review-or-audit-artifact attribution, disclosure of model/session/access-limits/head/verdict, a bounded correction plus exact-head re-review for material findings, and explicit principal acceptance before merge). Fable remains eligible; an independent ChatGPT session (or any other reviewer meeting every point) is equally eligible; a Sonnet session may never review work it authored itself. **Prospectively supersedes only `PI-0025` §I's model-name-specific reviewer clause** — every other `PI-0025` §I requirement (independent review, exact-head anchoring, retained attribution, bounded correction, principal acceptance, post-merge verification, factual `operations/WORKSTREAMS.yaml` sync) is unchanged; `PI-0025`'s file is not edited and its `status: Accepted` is not changed, following this repository's own narrow-supersession convention (`OPS-0003`/`OPS-0005` precedent — the superseded decision keeps its accepted status). PR #161 (the PI-0025-gated AVGO/AMD/MRVL/INTC implementation) may therefore be independently reviewed by an eligible ChatGPT session once OPS-0007 merges — this filing leaves PR #161 exactly as found (open, draft, unmerged) and performs no review itself. Defines a **PROVISIONAL** Intelligence status requiring, at minimum, all five of: eligible-reviewed under §1; any required bounded correction and exact-head re-review completed; explicit principal acceptance at the exact final head; **merged to authoritative `main` at that head**; and post-merge ancestry/scope/validator/test re-verification with `operations/WORKSTREAMS.yaml` factually synchronized — **independent review and principal acceptance alone do not qualify a batch** (an independently reviewed but unmerged draft, including PR #161 as it stands, is not PROVISIONAL); once so qualified, content is not yet subjected to WS-0005's later, deeper Milestone-4-9/eligible-reviewer audit — without altering the frozen Company Intelligence schema (`docs/PORTFOLIO_INTELLIGENCE_SPEC.md` §9/§20/§24) — recorded through review metadata, `operations/WORKSTREAMS.yaml`, and scenario-artifact labeling, not a new schema field. Authorizes, effective only on its own merge and only as later, separate, bounded implementation PRs neither opened nor performed by this filing: **(a)** one preliminary portfolio-role/tier/target-range-scenario architecture package built only from Intelligence that has cleared the full PROVISIONAL definition above (merged and post-merge-verified, not merely reviewed), explicitly labeled provisional, preserving current `targets.yaml` policy as an unchanged comparison baseline only, and never claiming Milestones 3-8 complete; and **(b)** one scenario-only, cash-only, zero-margin ($0 margin requested) Monday allocation-check package comparing an official `targets.yaml`-based run against a separately named scenario configuration — **may display the allocator's own ordinary advisory buy/trim/underweight/blocked output for both runs (display is not execution; no output is automatically authoritative and none may be automatically executed)** — with a reconciliation table, confidence/evidence flags, no `targets.yaml` mutation, no `holdings.yaml` change beyond an independently authorized live sync, and no order placement — built by calling `allocate.py`'s existing pure functions (`plan()`, `build_roster()`, confirmed by direct code inspection to already accept `targets` as an explicit parameter rather than reading the file internally) with an in-memory scenario config or an isolated throwaway copy, not by modifying `allocate.py` itself. Requires every provisional artifact to record its creation date, evidence cutoff, source Intelligence heads, holdings-sync date, market-data run time, assumptions, unresolved evidence, and expiration/supersession rule, with mandatory re-review on Fable-access return, material underlying-record change, expanded coverage, a material thesis-affecting event, or expiration — superseding a provisional conclusion later does not make the provisional work wasted. Does not authorize: a fourth Milestone 3 batch or any Milestone 4-9 execution; any tier/target/holdings/cluster/cap/margin-parameter change; any `allocate.py`/`margin_state.py` modification; any margin deployment; execution, routing, or automatic placement of any trade or order (advisory display of what the allocator would recommend, per (b) above, is not execution and is exactly what this decision authorizes); or treating a provisional scenario as automatically authoritative. Opened as a draft PR; not merged, not independently reviewed, in this session — the governance PR itself must be independently reviewed (by an eligible reviewer under its own §1 standard) and principal-accepted before merge, and its own merge is the sole event that makes §1's capability standard, §2's `PI-0025` narrowing, §3's PROVISIONAL category, and the §4/§5 later-PR authorizations effective. **Bounded correction (same day, this PR):** an independent ChatGPT review returned CHANGES REQUIRED — two Major findings (§7's blanket "recommend or execute a trade" prohibition conflicted with §5's own advisory allocation-check authorization; §§3/4/8 did not require PR #161 to merge and pass post-merge verification before its records could be treated as PROVISIONAL or feed §4/§5 work) and one Minor finding (stale WS-0005 register wording on Batch 2/PI-0025/Batch 3/Milestone 9) — resolved by one bounded correction: §5 and §7 now distinguish advisory display from trade execution explicitly; §§3/4/8 now require the full chain (review, correction if needed, re-review, principal acceptance, merge, post-merge verification) before PROVISIONAL status or §4/§5 use; `operations/WORKSTREAMS.yaml`'s stale passages were corrected to reflect PI-0025's and PR #159's actual merged state. Requires its own exact-head independent re-review before this decision may be considered ready.

  **Bounded correction (same day, this PR, review `4867365726`).** An independent exact-head review of PR #253 (anchored to head `3876c21e08c5a4793bbce59b0ea976b1d260d844`) returned CHANGES REQUIRED — 1 BLOCKING, 1 MAJOR, 1 MINOR, 2 NOTE (not actionable). **BLOCKING, resolved:** the sanitizer's marker list caught the adjective "gated" but never the bare policy noun "gate" — every one of the six formerly-gated records (SNPS, ICE, SPGI, WM, RKLB, TSLA) discusses its own `gates.yaml` entry using constructions like "this record's gate"/"the gate's own X"/"the gate names Y", none of which contained "gated", so that language reached all five drafting shards unredacted; a related dangling-cross-reference defect let ETN.md's own "see 'Governed policy' below" paragraph survive even though the section it named was correctly stripped; the "fail-closed complete-package scan" was also found tautological — it reran the exact same pattern list the strip pass had already applied to the already-redacted text, so nothing the strip pass missed could ever be caught. Root-cause-fixed, not patched per-ticker: `intelligence_classification_sanitizer.py` v1.1 adds a gate-policy detector (bare "gate"/"gates" with a legitimate-use whitelist for real non-policy usage independently confirmed in this corpus — ASML's "technological gate," KLAC's "gate-all-around" transistor structures, PWR's "customer-qualification gate," and OPS-0008 process/methodology gates like "stop-before-drafting gate"/"CI gate"/"source-readiness gate"); merges the whole-section-header title list into the paragraph-level strip pass so any dangling cross-reference to a stripped section is caught generically, not as a one-off; extends item-level YAML redaction to `catalysts[].catalyst` (previously entirely unscanned — SNPS's own catalyst-field leak reached its shard through exactly this gap); adds a genuinely independent second-stage verifier (`independent_policy_scan()`, built from materially different, broader concept checks — bare `conviction`, the gate-policy family, literal `gates.yaml`/`next_gate`/`allow_add`/`cash_pending_clearance` config-key names, a target/percent-of-book numeric pattern, chart-domain — used by both the post-strip re-scan and the final complete-package scan, replacing the tautological reuse); and fixes a discovered whitespace-normalization gap (`yaml.safe_dump` line-wraps long strings, splitting a multi-word whitelist phrase like "customer-qualification gate" across a newline and defeating the literal-space match — every check now runs against a whitespace-collapsed working copy). Independently re-derived, not assumed: exactly 8 of 27 sanitized packages were provably contaminated (byte-hash comparison between the original and corrected sanitizer) — the six formerly-gated names plus **ETN** (the dangling reference) and **PWR** (a genuine bare-`conviction` leak in `risks[2].risk`, unrelated to gate policy) — MSFT's own apparent change on first pass turned out to be a false positive (a legitimate "source-readiness gate" phrase not yet whitelisted), confirmed by re-running the diff after adding it. 27 new regression tests added (`test_intelligence_classification_sanitizer.py`), including per-ticker fail-closed checks for all six formerly-gated names, the ETN dangling-reference regression, a policy-synonym-absent-from-the-strip-list case, and explicit tests proving the strip and verify layers use different mechanisms. **MAJOR, resolved:** the original shard instruction's "worth a reviewer's specific attention" threshold for `capital_priority.status: case_for_review` was this implementation's own gloss, not grounded in `TIER-0002`'s controlling text, and — because virtually every record in this risk/catalyst-rich corpus can satisfy "worth attention" under a permissive reading — produced a 25-of-27 near-uniform result. Corrected instruction re-grounded directly in `TIER-0002` §3.4's own quoted language ("does additional-capital-deployment consideration exist... relative to current policy and available alternatives?") and `docs/INVESTMENT_ONTOLOGY.md` §E's exact definition, explicitly naming `maintain_current_weight` as the expected default and restricting `case_for_review` to a genuinely distinctive, specific development materially different in kind or degree from ordinary disclosed risk — with an explicit instruction not to target any particular distribution and no exposure to the prior 25/2 result or to this repository's historical committee conclusions (which would themselves have been an improper answer key). All 27 tickers' `capital_priority` were redrafted from fresh, isolated shard sessions with zero access to the prior result, using the corrected instruction and (for the 8 contaminated tickers) the corrected sanitized evidence; economic_role was redrafted only for those same 8 contaminated tickers, since the sanitized evidence the other 19 saw is provably byte-identical to what an already-accepted-shape blind session already judged. **Corrected result: 17 of 27 `case_for_review`, 9 `maintain_current_weight` (COST, WM, KLAC, PANW, TSM, MSFT, V, AMZN, PWR), 1 `no_assessment` (SPGI, whose own evidence base is now majority-redacted after the sanitizer fix — 4 of 7 risks, 2 catalysts, 2 sources, and the full thesis narrative all excluded — correctly reflected as insufficient evidence rather than forced into either determination)** — a textual-similarity scan of all 26 non-`no_assessment` rationales found a maximum pairwise similarity of 13%, confirming genuine per-ticker differentiation, not templating. **MINOR, resolved:** the PR body's "33 files" corrected to the actual, independently reconfirmed **34** (unchanged by this correction, which modifies only already-tracked files — no new file was added). - **August 2026** — **`XASSET-0002` proposed (not yet accepted): ETF + crypto blind-classification framework design** (`governance/decisions/XASSET-0002-etf-and-crypto-classification-framework-design.md`, supporting artifact `governance/audits/WS0014_ETF_CRYPTO_CLASSIFICATION_FRAMEWORK_DESIGN_20260807.md`). Independently re-confirmed `TIER-0013` (PR #267) merged before proceeding — merge commit `bb909532b9329e22d509180b2f308103f3594fa0` (`origin/main`'s current tip), `WS-0005` `status: complete`/`priority: secondary`, all nine `OPS-0006` §4 milestone gates `complete`, zero `priority: primary` workstreams remaining. On `XASSET-0001` §J's own explicit authority ("structural ETF + crypto framework design (step 3)... may reasonably batch as one architecture unit"), designs — as text only, not an authorization, not applied to any fund or coin — two asset-appropriate blind-classification frameworks, neither reusing `TIER-0002`'s company-shaped equity schema (`economic_role`/`capital_priority` have no coherent analogue for a fund with no issuer or a coin with no capital-allocation decision-maker, per `XASSET-0001` §C's explicit prohibition). **ETF framework**: six substantive axes (`structural_role`, `constituent_exposure` — merging underlying exposure/geography/sector/currency, one evidence source — `overlap_and_concentration` — a mechanical rollup of `issuer_lookthrough.yaml`, kept separate from `constituent_exposure` to preserve the judgment-vs-computation boundary — `cost_and_tracking_quality`, `liquidity`, `structure_and_methodology`) plus `evidence_quality`. **Crypto framework**: six substantive axes (`network_fundamentals` — protocol + adoption — `economic_model` — fee/supply/staking economics, each conditionally `not_applicable` per coin — `liquidity_and_market_structure`, `custody_and_counterparty_risk` — a risk class equities/ETFs don't carry — `correlation_and_volatility` — mechanical, no numeric coefficient field, cross-referenced only once a separately-authorized study exists — `regulatory_and_structural_uncertainty` — currently-disclosed matters only, explicitly barred from any predictive regulatory/legal forecast, matching CLAUDE.md's own no-predictive-research guardrail) plus `evidence_quality`. Every candidate axis named in the authorizing prompt was evaluated and either kept, merged (with reasoning), or explicitly excluded (equity-shaped concepts) — none omitted silently. **Shared cross-asset-handoff envelope** (`instrument_id`, `asset_type`, `schema_version`, `provenance`, `evidence_quality_status`, `uncertainty_summary`, `structural_risk_flags`, `record_status`, `valuation_and_economic_assessment_readiness`, `cross_asset_handoff`, `abstention_index`) — every summary field an explicit read-only copy of an already-computed axis value, never a new computation, the mechanism by which the design guarantees no hidden scoring. **No numeric score/rank/target field anywhere**, except one real, disclosed, inherited financial fact (`cost_and_tracking_quality.expense_ratio_pct`) — matching how Milestone 6/7/8 already carry inherited percentages without those being scores; tracking quality itself is represented categorically (`tracking_quality_category`), not as a second numeric field. `valuation_and_economic_assessment_readiness.status` forces exactly one value, `valuation_required`, today — the direct ETF/crypto analogue of `TIER-0009` §G.4/§G.5's forced equity valuation-abstention design — preserving framework/classification → later asset-specific valuation → later cross-asset synthesis → later sizing. **GLD resolved as Option C, no principal stop required**: three independently sufficient, mutually reinforcing lines of existing authority — `targets.yaml`'s own `asset_class: fund` tag on GLD (line 70, identical category to SPY/VEA/VWO); `issuer_lookthrough.yaml`'s own live data, independently re-verified this session, excluding GLD from every `funds:` entry across 20+ issuer rows (GLD holds physical gold, not equity constituents); and `XASSET-0001` §D's own explicit separation of GLD's *role* (functional doctrine, future, separate) from asset *structure* (classification, in-scope here) — GLD receives structural fund-mechanics evaluation now (its `constituent_exposure`/`overlap_and_concentration` axes are expected to resolve `not_applicable`, a genuine structural fact, not a schema failure), while its portfolio role (ballast vs. broad-market-beta) is explicitly reserved to a fully separate, future, functional cash/reserve/GLD/debt doctrine unit (`WS-0014` item 8) that this design does not perform. **Contamination analysis, not blindly copied from `TIER-0004`**: `TIER-0004`'s file-level redaction-and-reseal pipeline exists because Company Intelligence records literally embed policy content (`portfolio_role_ref`/`conviction`) in the same file a blind drafter reads — no ETF/crypto Intelligence record exists today and neither framework's evidence sources (fund prospectuses, protocol documentation) embed portfolio-policy text, so that pipeline does not transfer; judgment-before-mechanical-rollup sequencing does transfer (bias-avoidance, asset-shape-independent); multi-shard isolation is explicitly determined unnecessary at today's population scale (ETF ≤4, crypto = 3, versus 27 equities across five shards) — each determination is argued, not assumed. Specifies a thirteen-point validator specification and a ~24-item focused test inventory for a future, separate implementing PR, explicitly carrying forward three lessons from this repository's own prior validator-review history: closed-schema checks must reject extra keys, not just missing ones (`contender_registry_validator.py`'s own MAJOR finding); an independent second-stage check must use a materially different mechanism than the logic it verifies, not call the same function twice (`TIER-0004`'s own dangling-reference-backstop correction); a self-declared boolean flag is not a substitute for an independent free-text scan (`reconciliation_validator.py`'s own disclosed MINOR, already avoided once by `recommendation_validator.py`'s design). **Lane M folded in**: `CONTENDER-0002`'s own implementation PR (#258) independently re-confirmed fully merged (merge commit `1d5d93f94bbc39a0b9d99178a7b477e6f0f27928`), reviewed (final delta `pullrequestreview-4875587500`, APPROVED FOR PRINCIPAL EXACT-HEAD ACCEPTANCE), principal-accepted (`issuecomment-5205949519`), and post-merge verified (`issuecomment-5206063709`) — the `contender0002-normalization-and-readiness-screening-implementation` gate (`status: in_progress`, `pr: 258`) was accurate as filed but stale; per the established `TIER-0002`/`REL-0002`-`REL-0006`/`PI-0039`/`REL-0007`/`TIER-0007`/`TIER-0009`/`TIER-0011` convention, its own text is left unedited and one new, additive `contender0002-implementation-post-merge-verification` gate records the confirmed state instead. Adds one further additive `xasset0002-etf-crypto-framework-design` gate (`status: in_progress`, `pr: null` — this session does not mark its own unmerged work complete) and updates `WS-0014`'s `active_branch`/`active_pr`/`last_verified_main_sha`/`last_verified_date`/`blocker`/`next_action`/`authorized_by` fields — `WS-0014`'s own `status: proposed`/`priority: secondary` are unedited; `WS-0005` is unaffected. Four pre-registered research questions (archetype differentiation, left genuinely open per the task's own "do not silently force one method across structurally different companies" instruction; methodology defensibility and data requirements; false-precision-prevention requirements; evidence sufficiency against the existing Company Intelligence schema) are evaluated via a **closed 7×7 = 49-cell matrix** — seven methodology families (asset-based/balance-sheet-adjusted; FCFF DCF; FCFE DCF (with a dividend-discount/excess-return variant for financial intermediaries); earnings/FCF-yield screens; relative valuation/multiples; ROIC/reinvestment economics; scenario/probability-weighted analysis) against seven business-economics archetype categories (asset-light platform; capital-intensive infrastructure; financial intermediation; commodity/cyclical; early-stage/binary-outcome; diversified/multi-segment; mature stable compounder) — each cell resolved to one of a closed four-value vocabulary (`defensible` / `defensible_with_adjustment` / `not_defensible` / `insufficient_evidence_to_determine`), never a numeric score. The archetype taxonomy is explicitly, deliberately distinct from `docs/INVESTMENT_ONTOLOGY.md` (`ONTO-0001`)'s own economic-systems/company-roles/capital-types vocabulary (a different analytical purpose — thematic committee discussion, not methodology-selection criteria) and assigns **no real company to any category** — every example is generic, no canonical-roster ticker is named or classified anywhere in the protocol. **No numeric materiality threshold is invented** (protocol §14) — since this study produces no empirically-measured numeric outcome, manufacturing a NUM-0001-style threshold would itself be false precision; the 49-cell closed matrix, not a numeric run ceiling, is this study's bounded-trial mechanism. **False-precision protections** bind any future application phase (not authorized here): mandatory range outputs with disclosed sensitivity, a mandatory assumptions ledger with explicit provenance labeling, no fabricated decimal precision, and a first-class abstention path — matching this repository's existing `unable_to_determine`/forced-`valuation_required` abstention discipline (`TIER-0002`, `XASSET-0002`, `XASSET-0005`). **Absolute prohibitions restated**: no valuation, fair value, price target, or expected return for any real company at any point, including an "illustrative" application; no archetype-category assignment of any real company; no historical backtest of methodology output against subsequent price performance under any framing; no chart/technical-analysis input; no consumption of any Company Intelligence record's actual field content or any `targets.yaml`/`holdings.yaml`/`gates.yaml`/`issuer_lookthrough.yaml` field value as a research input; no ETF/crypto/GLD/cash-reserve/debt-reduction valuation methodology content (remains `WS-0014`/`XASSET-0001` §C/§D's own separate, unaffected scope); no claim that `TIER-0009`'s `valuation_required` status is resolved by this charter's own output.
