Multiple genuine cross-source numeric conflicts were caught and disclosed rather than silently resolved — including one apparent AI-search-synthesis fabrication (Visa's FY2025 net income, an initial $22.03B/EPS $11.22 pair rejected in favor of an internally-consistent $20.1B/EPS $10.20 pair after a sanity cross-check), a rejected aggregator claim (Amazon's total-debt figure, $209.88B, rejected as evidently wrong in favor of a directly-reported-component derivation showing a net cash position instead), and several disclosed-not-resolved discrepancies (PWR's and WM's FY2025 diluted EPS, AVGO's FY2025 total debt across two dated snapshots, GOOGL's current price/market cap). `financial_evidence` populates revenue and net income (`earnings`) for multiple fiscal years in all 27 records, with operating margin (derived, arithmetic shown), free cash flow, capex, cash, debt/net debt, and diluted share count concentrated on the most recent completed fiscal year where research supported it; every line item is honestly marked `value_basis: reported` or `derived`. `market_observed_evidence` populates a dated current share price for 25 of 27 records (SNPS and GEV explicitly abstained — WebSearch budget exhausted before a reliable price was located for either, a disclosed gap not an estimate). `segment_evidence` populates real dated segment data for 18 of 27 records; 9 abstain with a specific ticker-level reason (e.g. TSM discloses percentage-of-revenue node/platform mix rather than a dollar-value segment table the schema expects; GNRC's only located figures were identified as a likely mislabeled single quarter and explicitly rejected rather than used). `peer_set_evidence` populates 2-3 disclosed candidate peers per record, every one `inclusion_status: included` as a comparability candidate only — independently confirmed by direct scan that zero peer set anywhere is an applied/selected set for any valuation. `scenario_evidence` populates 1-3 factual, sourced, currently-known catalysts per record (NVDA's H20/export-control charge, RTX's GTF powder-metal issue, RKLB's pending Iridium acquisition, PANW's CyberArk acquisition, and others) with **zero `probability_weight` populated in any scenario for any real company anywhere**, independently confirmed by direct scan. `discount_rate_evidence` is abstained for all 27 records, uniformly and by design — this pass gathered financial-statement, market-price, segment, peer-candidate, and scenario evidence; it did not conduct dedicated discount-rate-component research (risk-free rate, cost of debt, tax rate, capital-structure weights, beta), a disclosed scope limitation rather than a schema gap, independently confirmed by direct scan that zero of the five discount-rate component keys appear populated anywhere.

**Bounded correction (same day, this PR).** An independent exact-head review (posted as a `COMMENT` on PR #291 — GitHub rejected `APPROVE`/`REQUEST_CHANGES` since the reviewing and authoring sessions share the same GitHub account, the same disclosed platform restriction this repository's history has repeatedly recorded; the verdict was treated with the same weight as a formal review) anchored to exact head `eaac705a32afb582728f32490d6ad0e0a02aea74` returned CHANGES REQUIRED — 0 BLOCKING / 3 MAJOR / 3 MINOR / 2 NOTE. All 27 tickers' mechanical backbone (hash/reference integrity, `governed_role` compatibility against the live 49-cell table, DCF/discount-rate non-fabrication, conflict propagation, domain-abstention echoing, evidence-quality honesty, protected-path isolation) was independently confirmed clean — every finding was a methodology-disclosure-quality gap, none a schema/authority/hash-integrity defect. **MAJOR-1, resolved**: the 7 `completed` SOTP (`family_1`) records' low/high sensitivity multipliers were used to compute the reported range but never logged to `assumptions_ledger` — a reader could not reproduce the range from the record's own disclosed inputs. Every segment across all 7 SOTP records (`AMZN`, `AVGO`, `GOOGL`, `MSFT`, `RTX`, `SPGI`, `TSLA`) now logs all three tiers (low/base/high), matching the `family_5` records' own pre-existing pattern. **MAJOR-2, resolved for 3 records, disclosed for 4**: every profit-bearing segment previously got an identical flat multiplier regardless of its own real, disclosed margin (AMZN's 35.4%-margin AWS and 6.9%-margin North America both got `15x`), mathematically collapsing several SOTP results to a single blended multiple applied via segment framing. For the 3 records with real per-segment profit evidence (`AMZN`, `MSFT`, `RTX`), each segment's own margin now selects its own EV/EBIT tier (`>=35%` high, `20-35%` mid, `<20%` low) — genuine, evidence-grounded differentiation (AWS now lands high-tier, NA/International low-tier; RTX's Collins/Pratt & Whitney land in the same low tier as a real finding, not an artifact, since both genuinely have similarly thin margins). For the 4 records with no segment-level profit disclosed anywhere in Stage-3 evidence (`AVGO`, `GOOGL`, `SPGI`, `TSLA`), no stronger evidence exists to differentiate further — each record's `uncertainty_summary` now states explicitly that the uniform tier makes the computation arithmetically equivalent to a single blended multiple, rather than leaving that consequence implicit. **MAJOR-3, resolved**: exactly 14 of 27 `family_5` records (`ASML`, `COST`, `ETN`, `ICE`, `ISRG`, `LLY`, `META`, `NVDA`, `PANW`, `PWR`, `SNPS`, `TMO`, `V`, `WM`) silently excluded one of three Stage-3-included peer candidates with zero disclosed rationale — a mechanical-truncation pattern (the third-listed candidate specifically, in 13 of 14 cases), directly contradicting this session's own retained audit §7 claim that peer selection was "not exercised." Resolved by including all Stage-3-included candidates in every affected record (no genuine comparability reason existed to exclude any of them), which also mechanically fixed `ICE.yaml`/`V.yaml`'s internal "three included peers" text inconsistency; the audit's §7 claim is corrected in a new §14. **MINOR-2, resolved**: `GNRC.yaml`/`KLAC.yaml` carried a leftover `"(for RKLB)"` phrase copy-pasted from `RKLB.yaml`'s own isolation-compliance sentence — split into a scenario-value-only isolation note (GNRC/KLAC) and a scenario-value-and-probability-weight note (RKLB only). **MINOR-3, resolved**: every `family_5` record's dollar base-metric ledger entry, previously misnamed `latest_fy_pe`/`latest_fy_pfcf` (a ratio-shaped name storing a dollar figure), renamed to `latest_fy_net_income`/`latest_fy_fcf`. **MINOR-1** (PR body's "Files changed (33)" header vs. the actual 32) is a PR-description text fix, not a repository-file change. **No `result_status`/`family_status` changed for any ticker** — result distribution independently reconfirmed unchanged at 18 `completed` / 9 `partial` / 0 `unable_to_determine`; these were disclosure-quality and methodology-honesty corrections.   **GLD's three named research questions (`XASSET-0008` §K) were answered with real, dated, sourced evidence via `WebSearch` aggregation, not defaulted to abstention**: `historical_equity_drawdown_behavior` → `historically_mixed`, grounded in three separate historical episodes (2008 Lehman collapse, March 2020 COVID crash, 2022 broad decline) — gold fell sharply alongside equities during both acute-panic phases (2008, 2020) before diverging positively in the months that followed, while showing comparative stability during 2022's non-acute-panic decline; a clean negative-correlation characterization was explicitly rejected as unsupported by the acute-panic co-decline pattern. `cost_and_tracking_economic_significance` → `elevated_vs_category`, grounded in a sourced comparison of GLD's own already-sealed expense ratio against three physically-backed peer gold ETFs in the same fund category (iShares Gold Trust/IAU, abrdn Physical Gold Shares/SGOL, SPDR Gold MiniShares/GLDM), all of which cost meaningfully less — the single secondary-sourced tracking-error estimate found (~0.30%) was deliberately **not** used as a determined finding, since it rests on one non-primary source and would require citing a bare numeric figure this schema's own zero-numeric rule forbids regardless of source. `historical_inflation_sensitivity` → `historically_mixed_or_inconsistent`, disclosing rather than silently reconciling genuinely divergent sourced material (several academic-summary sources describing a positive long-horizon, multi-decade hedging relationship; others describing the shorter-horizon correlation since the early 1970s as inconsistent and statistically unreliable). `CASH_LIKE_CAPITAL`'s own single axis (`deployability_and_optionality` → `high_optionality_low_friction`) required no external research, reasoned entirely from directly-inspected primary repository text (`CLAUDE.md`'s own Workflow/Portfolio Doctrine sections, `targets.yaml`, `gates.yaml`'s confirmed zero `RESERVE`/`GLD` mentions, `allocate.py`/`levels.py`'s own confirmed `("reserve", "cash")` single-tuple grouping) — never from either legacy record's own prior characterization, per `XASSET-0008` §C.2's own binding rule. **Zero abstentions across both sealed records** — a genuine evidence-driven outcome disclosed as such, not a default; the schema's own `unable_to_determine` abstention path remained fully available throughout drafting and would have been used for any field where adequate evidence was not located.

## Guardrails (enforce on me)
- Margin buffer below 30% → recommend immediate de-lever (trim or pay down margin), same severity as the cash-tight guardrail. Never propose *increasing* leverage as a response to a drawdown.
- Trades proposed outside a deposit cycle → remind me of the workflow before helping.
- Proposals to build new analysis/research/thesis systems → cite the Decisions Log first; the backtest showed added layers subtract value.
- Cash tight (recent withdrawals, sub-$500 buffer) → recommend inaction. **Pausing is the system working.**
- No predictive research, price targets, or "opportunity maps" — the tool computes opportunities at runtime on deposit day.
- Never hallucinate prices, dates, or holdings. Live data via the read-only Alpaca API (`alpaca_client.py`; there is no Alpaca MCP). `holdings.yaml` is truth for positions.
- **Verify before acting on external review.** Specific values, gate behaviors, and return values from a code/doctrine review done outside this session (a different chat, no live data or file access) must be confirmed by running the actual code or checking the actual file before committing to them — assume an outside inference is wrong until verified. Demonstrated twice in one session: a reviewer's AAPL price (~$215) was stale vs. the live $314.93, and a reviewer's assumed RSI-of-flat-series value (50) was wrong vs. the code's actual, correct 100.0 (`avg_loss == 0` branch). Applies to claims *about* this repo from outside it, not to the live Alpaca data or files already used as source of truth.
- **Earnings gate: live as of 2026-07-15.** Was blocked by the remote environment's network policy (yfinance/Yahoo domains not allowlisted) → degraded to `earnings:unavailable` flags, requiring manual web-search verification on every flagged buy. Fixed permanently: (1) allowlisted `fc.yahoo.com` + `query1/query2.finance.yahoo.com` in the environment's network policy; (2) `earnings.py` now passes yfinance a `curl_cffi` session pinned to `impersonate="chrome110"` — the default `impersonate="chrome"` (latest) fingerprint was getting connection-reset by this environment's proxy even with the domains allowed; the older fingerprint passes clean. Verified live: AAPL/NVDA/MSFT/GOOGL/TSLA all returned real dates, correctly gating GOOGL/TSLA (earnings exactly 7 days out — right at the blackout line). The manual web-search fallback is no longer needed but stays as a backstop if `earnings:unavailable` ever reappears (e.g. Yahoo API changes).
- **Earnings gate, full-roster audit (2026-07-15):** ran all 65 tickers through the gate to check coverage, found two more issues beyond the fix above. (1) **Network-policy gap, still open** — yfinance's primary source (`get_earnings_dates`) hits bare `finance.yahoo.com`, which was never added to the allowlist (only `query1/query2.finance.yahoo.com` + `fc.yahoo.com` were). Confirmed via the proxy's own relay-failure log: repeated 403 policy denials on `finance.yahoo.com:443`. Every lookup has silently been running on the weaker `calendar` fallback (single upcoming date only, no history) instead of the primary source — the gate still mostly works but on thinner data than intended. Not fixed yet; needs `finance.yahoo.com` added to the environment's network policy alongside the existing two hosts. (2) **BRK.B symbol mismatch — fixed.** Yahoo's symbol is `BRK-B` (dash), not `BRK.B` (dot, our brokerage-convention symbol) — Yahoo returned an empty calendar for the dot form. `earnings.py` now translates via a `_YAHOO_SYMBOL` map before lookup; verified BRK.B → 2026-08-01. (3) **JPM `unavailable` — not a bug, self-resolving.** JPM reported earnings 2026-07-14 (day before this audit); Yahoo's calendar hadn't posted the next date yet, so both primary and fallback correctly had nothing forward-looking to return. Expected to populate within days.

## Open Items
- ~~T1/T2 have no trim rule (return question)~~ — **backtested and closed 2026-07-15** (`reports/t1t2_trim_backtest.md`, Decisions Log), verdict NO CHANGE on TWR/aggregate-MaxDD grounds. Not reopenable without a new regime in the data.
- ~~T1 concentration, capital-impairment question~~ — **decided 2026-07-15: T1/T2 concentration ceiling added, 1.5x tier target, mechanical, no RSI gate, floored at target.** Same treatment as the cluster caps (`allocate.py`'s `plan()`, `targets.yaml`'s `gates.t1t2_trim_mult`). This is a **doctrine decision, not a backtest verdict** — explicitly the same category as the 1.8x leverage cap and 30% buffer floor: those are "no discretion, no timing, no exceptions" ceilings because the cost of a wrong tail call outweighs flexibility's upside, and a single T1/T2 name at 2x+ target under leverage is the same risk shape at smaller scale (NVDA's 2.14x/-66.4% decomposition in `t1t2_trim_backtest.md` is the disqualifying condition trim_backtest.md's leverage override already established — applying it here isn't a new judgment, it's the existing standard).
  **Important precision note, not just a footnote: what got built is NOT what got backtested.** `t1t2_trim_backtest.md`'s Arm B/C tested a *ceiling-style* trim — excess above 1.5x trimmed back down to exactly 1.5x — and measured ~0.02pp cost. The live code instead does a *floor-at-target* trim (down to 1.0x), matching the cluster-cap mechanism exactly, because that's what "same as the cluster caps" means in this codebase. Floor-at-target removes roughly 1.5x more dollars per breach than the tested ceiling-style version, every time, permanently. **The ~0.02pp/"basically free" number does not apply to the rule actually running in production** — it applies to a smaller intervention that was tested but not shipped. This was a deliberate choice (matching existing cap mechanics over inventing a second trim shape) made and disclosed at build time, not an oversight, but it means this rule's cost is **untested, not "tested and cheap."** It's justified purely on doctrine grounds (same as the 1.8x cap and 30% floor: state it, apply it, don't relitigate without a new leverage regime) — not on the backtest's return number. Do not cite this entry as "backtest-verified, 0.02pp cost."
  **Live effect, first `--review` run:** 9 T1/T2 names crossed the ceiling — GOOGL, META, GEV, LLY, V, AMZN, ISRG, WMT, COST (~$1,044 total), confirming the underlying drift was real. That run's book, however, was later found to be stale — margin debt hadn't been re-synced after the trims executed and paid down margin ($2,671.85 → $1,628.64 confirmed via Robinhood screens), so the book was understated by ~$1,041 and several *additional* trims (CRWD, PANW, BABA, SPCX, INTC/semis cap) that appeared on a follow-up run were artifacts of that stale book, not real signals — they disappeared entirely once margin was re-synced to the correct debt/buffer (61.77%, Robinhood-displayed). The original 9-name list itself was unaffected by this correction (all confirmed filled independently via order-history screenshots), but it's a reminder that this rule's *outputs* are only as good as the margin sync discipline the workflow already calls for — a mechanical trim rule interacting with a stale book will manufacture false signals mechanically, same as it would flag real ones.

- **Live-priced book only tracks the regular session, 9:30am–4:00pm ET — frozen outside it, both sides** (confirmed 2026-07-13 post-close, confirmed 2026-07-14 pre-market): Alpaca's free daily bars reflect the official close, not extended-hours trades. During market hours the book tracks Robinhood almost exactly (confirmed within pennies same day). After the 4pm bell, Robinhood keeps moving with after-hours activity while `resolve_holdings()` holds flat at the close print — a gap that opened to ~$8.56 within one minute of close on a volatile after-hours session. Same thing pre-market: at 9:06am ET (24 min before open), a live Robinhood check showed a $23 (0.39%) gap while the book was still anchored to the prior day's close, with JPM reporting earnings live pre-market at the time. Not fixable without a paid real-time/extended-hours feed — unlike the SKHY gap, this one is structural to the free daily-bar feed, not a settling-in period. The gap should close back to penny-level accuracy shortly after the 9:30am open once today's regular-session bar starts updating.

## Formatting
Extremely concise. Bold headers, tables for comparisons, zero preamble. Honesty over comfort — name rationalizations directly.

Final activation remains the external one-shot runtime attestation and the operator's act, never another merged activation PR. Packaging is **one authorization → one coherent implementation PR**, never one per component; a session finding a concrete technical reason otherwise must **stop and disclose**. Effective only on **complete** closure of all **seven** conditions, including successful merge-commit CI at the exact merge SHA — `XASSET-0035`'s own lifecycle-enumeration omission is not repeated. 108 adversarial tests, **40 injected decision overreaches across three rounds all caught** with the file restored byte-identical, **zero** `or`-fallback assertions, and AST guards proving no filesystem write and no `risk_lane_boundary` reference. **Bounded correction (same PR, independent FULL review `4952475219`, CHANGES REQUIRED — 0 BLOCKING / 1 MAJOR / 1 MINOR / 1 NOTE):** the reviewer accepted the core authority determination in full — the grant, the §P.1 separation, the `XASSET-0029` activation model, the steps-2–7 scope, the steps-8–11 withholding, and the seven-condition effectivity lifecycle are all unchanged. **MAJOR 1** — the original §F stated one categorical rule (validate the runner only against synthetic or isolated inputs; never over the real 680-construction universe), conflating **reading already-frozen construction identities** with **deriving Stage-1 outcomes from them**, and making the runner's most important integrity property — that the real production traversal consumes exactly the frozen 680 in exact canonical order with no duplicate/missing/extra/relabelled identity — unprovable at its real boundary. Resolved by new **§F.1**, a two-sided boundary: **§F.1(a) authorizes** read-only, non-outcome-producing structural traversal of the real frozen universe, expressly including through the actual production seam rather than only a synthetic lookalike, to prove exact-680 coverage, canonical ordering, duplicate/missing/extra rejection, identity immutability and no relabelling, the 680/48/frozen-hash verification, and B2's 480/200 classification recomputed from frozen identities — the permission **conditional** on remaining read-only and non-outcome-producing and never extending past that condition; **§F.1(b) keeps absolutely prohibited** every outcome-producing act (gate evaluation for a real registered construction, disposition/cell/roll-up derivation, result serialization or persistence, `stage1_results.yaml`, `AUTHORIZATION_ROOT` mutation, attestation, `ATTEMPT_1` claim, lane/ledger writes, data acquisition, protected `RISK` access), with an actual Stage-1 run over the real 680 still downstream of rebinding → attestation → `READY` → lawful claim → execution. **The one-shot `ATTEMPT_1` protection is not weakened by one step** — the correction narrows an over-broad ban to the precise act that would actually consume the lane, grounded in what this program already did (`XASSET-0035` recomputed the B2 480/200 inventory directly from the merged frozen universe; neither that nor this filing's own 680/48/hash preflight consumed `ATTEMPT_1`, because neither evaluated a gate). Two genuine suite gaps were found and closed rather than papered over — bare-phrase prohibition assertions survived qualifier splicing, so every §F.1(b) prohibition is now pinned with its own list terminator and the list lead-in separately. **MINOR 1** was a PR-body factual contradiction only ("production config" in the absolute withheld summary while the decision correctly authorizes the §G.B steps 2/3/5 production-configuration changes), corrected in the PR body; independently verified that this pointer, `operations/WORKSTREAMS.yaml`, and the decision file never carried it — in all three, "production configuration change" appears only inside the correct §P.1 reconciliation. No governance decision, canonical byte, validator, load-bearing path, universe value, or catalog count changed in the correction.
